# Cerid master plan

<!-- doc-role: tracker -->
> Only live slice/finding tracker. Current work: [ROADMAP](ROADMAP.md); current rules: [AGENTS](../AGENTS.md).

**This is the only live slice/subslice tracker.** Updated 2026-09-14 with the DIAG programme.
Current focus is the single pointer in [context](../context.md); this table alone owns live state.
The last shipped execution milestone is [CEIR-35](sessions/2026-09-11-ceir-35z-band-close.md).
Agent entry: [START_HERE](../START_HERE.md). Broad architecture/gaps:
[whole-system review](research/2026-09-12-cerid-whole-system-review.md); every G01–G30 finding has owners in this table.

## Purpose

Build the fully authorable, performant, cross-API renderer; the complete Cerid-owned UI; and **CR-D007**, the game
and engineering application editor. Algorithms, shaders, pipelines, frame graphs and behaviour are editable assets:
CHIR authors high-level behaviour, CEIR executes, CKIR expresses device kernels. The editor is a consumer of public
modules. Standalone games, engineering apps, the notebook and later DAWs use those same modules.

## Confirmed order and decisions

Recorded in [ADR-0129](decisions/0129-renderer-ui-editor-delivery-order.md).

**2026-09-14 user addition:** immediately after REPO.DEV, complete the **DIAG** runtime diagnostic foundation,
then the retained repository close and renderer sequence below. [ADR-0133](decisions/0133-runtime-diagnostics-and-instrumentation.md)
and the [DIAG contract](design/runtime-diagnostics.md) define all children; the [research](research/2026-09-14-diagnostics-and-instrumentation.md)
maps DG01–DG18 source findings. Existing CI/review gates remain. MAC.DIAG/WEB.DIAG qualify later-platform integration;
the early foundation must not depend on a future editor or claim universal bug detection.

1. Complete the **entire retained renderer feature library**, including virtual geometry, hybrid GI, neural rendering
   dependencies and OFF-1…9 production offline rendering. This is the user's explicit choice on 2026-09-12.
2. Build full `crd-ui` and CR-D007. Bootstrap the shell at I2D-4, then build the remaining widgets within it.
3. Finish hesap GPU and the MATLAB-like notebook, as a CR-D007 workspace **and** a thin standalone host.
4. Resume media, audio/plugins, later applications and preserved engine/domain work. Windows/Linux qualify first;
   macOS and web follow explicitly. Physics/geometry and unrelated code are untouched by this documentation audit.

The expanded AI programme qualifies **training and inference together**, including scoped full training, fine-tuning,
distillation and specialized game/product agents. Neural-renderer dependencies qualify before UI. Collaboration uses
an authoritative project host with local/offline recovery; multiplayer has a separate authoritative simulation contract.
[ADR-0130](decisions/0130-system-qualification-and-agent-driven-products.md) distinguishes confirmed product directions
from proposed mechanism choices. Broader modeling, DAW, CAD/CAM/EDA, embedded, digital-twin and hardware work remains
in the same sequence with explicit qualification children; no retained ID was removed.

Renderer `.runtime` rows finish the full runtime/asset work before UI. `.authoring` children then prove those same
features in CR-D007; the original family ID closes only afterward. This preserves the visual-authoring requirement
without pretending an editor exists before UI. Media codec IDs remain MED-1…12; MED-GPU-* identifies the separate
architecture contracts that formerly reused the same numbers.

**Still to review before implementation:** RAH-0's refreshed no-loss design and ADR-0107's full proposal. The language,
service and qualification recommendations are concrete in the execution contract. The audit request authorizes
documentation changes, not an invented ADR acceptance. No unavailable advisor sign-off is claimed. The old CEIR loop
grant ended with CEIR-35; this table does not silently reinstate it.

## How to use this document

Read [context](../context.md) for the current pointer, then this table. Open only the row's detail and ADR for work.
The [execution contract](design/renderer-ui-execution-contract.md) defines ownership, quality, publishing and UI gates.
The [inherited catalogue](design/rendering-ui-contracts.md) contains the complete feature lists; a short table cell
does not reduce them. The [audit](research/2026-09-12-system-audit.md) contains findings, evidence and module inventory.
[Systems](systems/README.md) maps the code; [decisions](decisions/README.md) records architecture.

**States:** Done = complete contract with evidence and sequential acceptance; In progress = the sole current row;
Blocked = named unmet non-CI gate; Needs CI = available local work finished, published CI output still required;
Recorded = dated evidence/documentation, not a new test run; Review = unresolved census/decision;
Open = unimplemented/unqualified contract; Partial = retained work/evidence awaiting full sequential acceptance;
Later = retained outside the current
renderer/UI/science delivery. Source plans and archives never carry live status. Capability maturity is a separate
machine-derived fact in [the registry](capabilities/gpu-platform-capabilities.toml), not inferred from these states.

Query `python scripts/check-master-plan.py --slice RAH-1`, `--find A24`, or `--next` for a small view of this same table.
Finding IDs link directly to evidence; the linked owner rows carry their live state. No second bug backlog is allowed.

## Strict sequential execution

**Latest user direction, 2026-09-12:** start at the earliest unfinished work unless explicitly directed to a future
slice. Leave no gaps. If evidence needs CI output, say so and continue available work. This supersedes the earlier
same-day rule that stopped all advancement while CI waited; other scope, hardware and renderer review gates remain.

1. Walk the Order column. Done and historical Recorded rows require no implementation; Needs CI rows retain an
   external verification gate. The first remaining row is the default work position, including Open, Partial, Review
   and Blocked. Context points there; only that row may be In progress. Finish all available work in that row first.
2. **Needs CI is not Done.** Use it only when all presently available local work is finished and published CI output
   is the remaining evidence. Link the actual run and dated local proof in the row. Say "needs CI output" and continue
   the next available work; never drop its requirements, weaken an oracle or claim an unobserved pass. Inspect changed
   CI results first on re-entry: a real failure returns to its owning row and takes priority in table order.
3. An explicit user request to work on a future slice overrides default scheduling, not its prerequisites/quality.
   Record the exact user direction in a session and link it in that row as `[User direction](sessions/<record>.md)`
   with `<!-- user-order: sessions/<record>.md -->`. This marker may be added only for a real explicit instruction,
   never inferred from a quality goal, old grant or agent-written plan. Return to earliest work after that assignment.
4. All full contracts, children, tests, publication/provider evidence and documentation remain required for Done.
   A pending prerequisite is not waived by continued work. Parents close only when every child and inherited gate
   closes. Hardware/design/scope blockers remain binding unless the user explicitly changes their contract.
5. Add a proven missing prerequisite before its owning unfinished row, with references and rationale. Never move
   unrelated work forward, reorder the table to conceal a gap or reclassify unfinished implementation as Recorded.
   New findings keep owning rows and exact evidence; CI waits and parked work are visible in this same table.
6. Previously parked later work remains Partial with its implementation/evidence intact. At its turn audit the full
   contract; reuse valid proof and run missing or newly justified checks. The validator enforces the default order,
   one active position, referenced user overrides and explicitly retained CI waits. No second status queue is allowed.

The [initial order correction](sessions/2026-09-12-strict-slice-order.md) preserves earlier evidence; the
[CI continuation amendment](sessions/2026-09-12-ci-continuation-and-dx12-workloads.md) records the latest direction.
New child IDs are proposed execution decomposition, clearly distinct from original source IDs. Add every additional
child here before implementing it; never create another tracker. A large row starts with a detailed reuse/design/
test note linked from its contract cell. Parent completion requires all children and inherited requirements.

| 051 | <a id="slice-repo.dev.5"></a>**REPO.DEV.5** | Done | Three-tier CI with one coverage contract: `.github/ci-tiers.json` gives every visible preset an owner (validated at configure, in preflight and by the tooling tests); preflight resolves the tier from the changed paths (documentation → preflight, sources → change, build system/workflow/scripts/register or an unknown base → complete; nightly and manual runs → complete) and every heavy job takes its matrix from that resolution; `required` is the one stable status and fails on a skipped expected job or a failed preflight; cancellation is limited to superseded pull-request runs; every lane records its CTest inventory, JUnit, the two-sided register gate and a `conclusion.json` bundle with revision, tree, toolchain and adapter identity. The five never-hosted presets (`win-relwithdebinfo`, `win-debug-scalar`, `win-shipping-profile`, `linux-gcc-debug-scalar`, native `win-vs`) join the complete tier. Bounded CTest parallelism and shards wait for the first complete-tier durations. The introducing push resolves to the complete tier. **Hosted (2026-10-06 audit):** the first all-green complete-tier run, 37311671924 at `4737877d` (25 of 25 jobs): preflight resolved to complete, `required` green with every expected job, an evidence bundle per lane (`conclusion.json` with revision, tree, toolchain and adapter), and the five never-hosted presets plus the native solution building and testing. | [REPO.DEV.4](#slice-repo.dev.4) | [Detail](design/ci-tiers.md); [Research](research/2026-09-12-large-cpp-development-and-ci.md#ci-design); [ADR-0001](decisions/0001-build-language.md); [Evidence](sessions/2026-09-14-ci-tiers.md); [Published CI](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/34780682504); [Repair](sessions/2026-09-14-first-complete-tier-run-repairs.md); [Hosted run 2026-10-05](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/37311671924); [Needs CI audit](sessions/2026-10-06-needs-ci-audit.md) |
| 052 | <a id="slice-repo.dev.6"></a>**REPO.DEV.6** | Done | One registry (`cmake/pins.json`) names every external input with version, commit-addressed source, SHA-256 and license: the nine CPM packages acquire as verified archives (`crd_add_pinned_package`, `CRD_INPUT_ARCHIVES` for verified offline copies), the CPM bootstrap, Vulkan SDK, Vulkan headers, SPIRV-Reflect, WARP and actionlint acquire through helpers that download to a partial file and verify before the final name exists, the imgui Vulkan patch applies to a build-owned copy from a versioned spec (the shared cache stays pristine, a drifted anchor fails the configure), every workflow action is a commit with its tag, every runner an explicit image, every checkout `persist-credentials: false`; `check-pins.py` guards the registry at configure, in preflight and in the tooling tests. Local proof: the full `win-debug` graph unchanged except the patched path, Linux reconfigured, four consumers on one source cache with the tree hash identical after the third and the fourth; hosted lanes on the next push. **Hosted (2026-10-06 audit):** not yet shown. Run 37311671924 verified the Vulkan headers, the validation layer and sccache on download, but every lane restored its CPM archive cache (and Windows its Vulkan SDK cache) through the restore-key fallback, as did the first complete run; cold acquisition of every archive, the Vulkan SDK and WARP through the verifying helpers has never run hosted. Needs one cache-cold hosted run. **Cold lanes landed (2026-10-06, user decision: a standing step, option 2):** the complete-tier `win-debug-scalar` and `linux-gcc-debug-scalar` skip every archive cache (`CRD_COLD_PRESETS`); `check-cold-acquisition.py` fails unless the CPM cache starts empty and every package the configure added is pinned and acquired (the Windows lane also installs the Vulkan SDK and the pinned WARP); proven by a real cold configure on the WSL reference host and its tooling test. The next complete-tier run is the hosted proof. **Hosted (2026-10-06, complete run 37395083192):** both cold lanes started from an empty cache. Each acquired all seven packages any configuration selects (Catch2, glfw, zstd, stb, cgltf, MikkTSpace, imgui), verified each against its pinned SHA-256, and passed its whole test lane. The Windows lane also installed the Vulkan SDK 1.4.341.1 and WARP 1.0.20 cold through their verifying helpers. Not yet shown: the two gated pins (Eigen and OpenBLAS, `CRD_BUILD_HESAP_VS_REFERENCE`, which no hosted preset builds), named but never acquired. Rather than leave them unproven, both cold lanes now also download and verify every unselected pin's archive (`check-cold-acquisition.py --fetch-unselected`, through `pins.download`). Both archives verify on the reference host. The next complete-tier run shows the remaining clause. | 064 | <a id="slice-diag.3a"></a>**DIAG.3a** | Needs CI | Complete every current allocator contract: checked size/alignment/range arithmetic, OOM/realloc and ownership; DG02/DG03. Core landed on win-debug (`crd/memory/checked_math.hpp`): constexpr overflow-checked `checked_add`/`checked_mul`/`checked_array_size`/`checked_align_up`/`checked_page_count`/`checked_padded_array_size`, each reporting overflow rather than wrapping (align-up refuses the near-SIZE_MAX wrap the naive form suffers; non-power-of-two alignment rejected). 31 assertions incl. compile-time static_asserts + boundary overflow. Now WIRED into real size paths: `PoolAllocator` slot-pad × count (CRD_FATAL on overflow, always-on) and `LinearAllocator` offset arithmetic (overflow fails like exhaustion, preserving the prior allocation) — contract test proves the near-SIZE_MAX request returns nullptr and the next in-bounds allocation still succeeds. Remaining (next iterations): the remaining allocators' size paths + the full per-allocator zero-size/exhaustion/realloc-failure sweep. **2026-10-06 audit:** reclassified from Needs CI, because the row's own remaining work (the other allocators' size paths and the full per-allocator sweep) is local work. **2026-10-06 boundary sweep:** one contract across all 12 `IAllocator`s (huge sizes and a 2^62 alignment must return nullptr from `try_allocate`, earlier blocks keep their bytes, foreign pointers are not owned) found wrap or assert defects in 9 (the POSIX malloc wrap only on Linux). Fixed with checked arithmetic in TLSF, growable TLSF, growable linear, POSIX malloc, the streaming budget check and the diagnostic decorator's redzone sizing, plus non-fatal `try_allocate` overrides on pool, growable pool and the decorator. The memory test exe sets the sanitizer default `allocator_may_return_null=1` (libc's contract; detection unchanged). All 12 pass on win-debug, win-shipping, win-clang-cl-shipping and win-asan, and on WSL clang TSan, gcc debug and gcc ASan; teeth were verified. **Part 2 (same day):** zero-size `try_allocate` returns nullptr on all 12 (the default `try_allocate` now refuses before `allocate`'s assert). Exhaustion refuses and keeps earlier blocks (linear, stack, pool, TLSF, virtual memory). A failed `reallocate` keeps the old block (the default `reallocate` copied into nullptr and freed it). TLSF `reallocate(p, 64, SIZE_MAX)` wrapped to 0 and split the live block; it now reaches the out-of-memory fatal, which the new `crd-diag-alloc-fatal-specimen` proves as a bounded child in three modes. Teeth were verified for all three fixes. **Part 3:** `OffsetAllocator` refuses a split with an exhausted node pool (it overran `m_free_nodes` in release builds) and refuses foreign, stale or double-freed handles in every build (`free` indexed `m_nodes` unchecked). `RingAllocator::try_claim` refuses an alignment the buffer cannot honour. Teeth were verified. **Part 4:** a block freed into the wrong allocator, over every ordered pair of the 12, is refused (pool, growable pool and TLSF used to adopt it in release builds, handing foreign memory out again), ignored by the arenas, or reported by the decorator; `MallocAllocator` cannot tell (documented limit). TLSF also refuses an immediate double free. Child arenas, nested chains and moved growable pools return exactly what they took from a decorated parent; arenas over external buffers stay inside them. Partial construction: owning arenas whose parent refuses now reach their own fatal instead of building on null (six specimen modes), the growable pool refuses a page non-fatally, and `construct`/`construct_array` handle refusal, count overflow and throwing constructors. Teeth were verified; 153 memory cases pass on win-debug, win-asan and the three WSL lanes (152 on both shipping lanes). Remaining: a hosted run showing the memory suite green on every lane, including the complete tier's `linux-clang-tsan`. | [DIAG.2b](#slice-diag.2b) | [Detail](design/runtime-diagnostics.md#diag-3a); [ADR-0133](decisions/0133-runtime-diagnostics-and-instrumentation.md); [Session](sessions/2026-09-14-diag-2-3-events-recorder-checked-math.md); [Published CI](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/34880793407); [Hosted run 2026-10-05](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/37311671924); [Needs CI audit](sessions/2026-10-06-needs-ci-audit.md); [Boundary sweep](sessions/2026-10-06-diag-3a-allocator-boundaries.md) |

No false deadlock claim on the four non-fault shapes: long progressing work reads Progressing; a badly overrun watchdog window is `Paused` (re-baselined, and the livelock check is skipped for it) covering debugger pause + suspend/resume; clock changes are excluded by construction (every interval is a `steady_clock` delta). Uncatchable termination is honest: `SIGKILL` runs no handler, writes no record, and a pre-existing record is retained (exit 137). stuck flush bounded to 152 ms; OOM-during-formatting **declared** (throwing allocator → counted drop via `dispatch`'s catch, or nullptr → `String::grow_to` null-deref → crash capture — never a hang; untested, no allocation-failure seam). **(d) reentrancy + lifecycle:** a `tl_in_sink_delivery` guard makes an assert fired inside a sink a suppressed, counted event (`sink_reentrant_assert_count()`) not a `sinks_mutex` self-deadlock (async + sync); assert before-init/after-shutdown bypass the logger; a shutdown-race async push is a counted drop. **(e) logger failure policy:** a throwing sink is a per-sink counted failure (`sink_failure_count()`), never `std::terminate` on the noexcept worker nor a lost record for the other sinks; queue pressure = newest-dropped (`dropped_count()`), Critical retained via sync bypass; sink lifetime = logger lifetime; missing console fails silently. **(f) labels + audit:** `RingBufferSink` labeled NOT crash-safe (allocates per record; structural proof — no crd crash/signal handler logs, crash specimens link no crd-log); the assert-ignore table is now a bounded FIFO ring (`ignore_assert_site` + `assert_ignore_eviction_count`, one stderr line on first eviction) instead of silent-when-full; shipping input-rejection audit **clean** — the toml parser rejects malformed input via runtime `parse_error` (proven on win-shipping, asserts off), consumer asserts (config/profile/perf) are preconditions/writer-invariants. Lanes: win-debug + **win-shipping** (`/W4 /WX`, incl. `[ignore]` + toml `[toml]` asserts-off) + `linux-gcc-debug` `-Werror`; (b) regression-checked on win-asan `[crash-capture]` 11/25 (the `[crash]` cases 5a already counts are unchanged); **no sanitizer lane by design** (5c instruments no allocator/UB — per (a)'s regime). Verified: `crd-core-tests` 440/28 (`[ignore]` 304), `crd-log-tests` 78/26 (`[flush]`/`[reentrancy]`/`[sinkfail]`), toml 36/6; both validators PASS. Named limitations (per 5a/5b precedent): no Windows minidump from a headless `abort()` (needs an SEH raise — future); unbounded `flush()` + logger-API-re-entry-from-a-sink are declared contract violations; `drop_on_overflow=false` blocks unbounded (configured); the shutdown-race running-gate is now checked under `queue_mutex` and exercised (2026-10-06: a lost stop wakeup in `shutdown()` hung `linux-clang-tsan` run 37427716844; fixed with bounded regression cases, see the lost-wakeup session); OOM untested (no seam); the ignore table is interactive/opt-in; no `remove_sink`; a fully post-shutdown log is silently discarded. | [DIAG.5b](#slice-diag.5b) | [Detail](design/runtime-diagnostics.md#diag-5c); [ADR-0133](decisions/0133-runtime-diagnostics-and-instrumentation.md); [Session](sessions/2026-09-15-diag-5c-assert-logging-policy.md); [Lost-wakeup fix](sessions/2026-10-06-log-shutdown-lost-wakeup.md); [Published CI](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/34946718738) |
| 076 | <a id="slice-diag.5d"></a>**DIAG.5d** | Needs CI | Versioned bounded crash/failure bundles, exact symbol identities, unloaded generations and adversarial import; DG14. Landed across (b)–(f) in `crd-perf` (`crd::` containers only): a **`cerid-diagnostics/1` bundle** — `BundleWriter`/`read_bundle` with a crc'd 40-byte header + per-section tag/flags/length/crc, explicit **absent** sections, honest **truncation** markers and **partial-section recovery** (`RecoveredTruncated`); **atomic publish** (`write_bundle_atomic`: tmp → flush-to-stable → rename, no leftover `.tmp`) and **retention** (`enforce_bundle_retention`, oldest-first eviction; ADR ≤256 MiB / ≤10 bundles). **Exact symbol identity**: a crd-native minidump byte-parser reads **every** module's RSDS **GUID/age + PDB name** and the **UnloadedModuleListStream** generation (a one-flag `MiniDumpWithUnloadedModules` add proved on a **real two-generation winhttp dump**); `identity_matches` **rejects wrong** GUID/age/build-id instead of guessing; `debug_file_path` builds the **symbol-store** (PDB by GUID/age) and Linux **`.build-id/<nn>/<rest>.debug`** conventions; **POSIX ELF build-id** captured at `install()` (async-signal-safe copy). **Trustworthy import**: `import_bundle` is a bounded, non-executing reader (borrows views, decodes POD, `noexcept`) that rejects oversized / too-many-sections (declared count peeked **before** the walk) / schema-mismatch / unsupported-version, flags oversized sections, refuses compressed payloads (v1 decompression-bomb defence), detects duplicate tags and **counts path-traversal** names; a **Manifest** (tag 1) that is redaction-honest **by design** (ID-8: no field/bit/API can declare the raw dump redacted; unknown flags reported, never interpreted); a **golden v1** byte-frozen migration guard. **Adversarial corpus + fuzz** (e2): a 10-seed committed corpus (truncations, traversal, oversized-length, decompression-bomb, wrong-schema, hostile `section_count`) replayed by `crd-fuzz-perf-bundle-corpus` on **every lane** and fuzzed under `CRD_ENABLE_FUZZER` (**libFuzzer 1,659,856 execs, 0 crashes** on linux-clang-fuzz). **Fresh-machine symbolization proven end-to-end** on a real dump (f): specimen → dump → `build_symbol_index_from_minidump` → bundle → `import_bundle` → every identity-bearing module resolves to a safe non-empty `debug_file_path` (a `None`-identity module resolves to `""` — refuse, don't guess), an unloaded generation survives, `age+1` is rejected. The frame→line **symbolizer is the offline consumer by design** (5b's rule); the **CLI executable** is routed to **DIAG.6c** (the bounded non-executing *library* is here); **dSYM/WASM** identities and an **N-1 reader** are named future work; **Linux unloaded-module attribution** is a named limitation (no OS-native list). Verified: perf `[symbols],[bundle],[import],[e2e]` **win-debug 405/32 + win-asan 332/32**, fuzz replay green win-debug/win-asan, core `[unloaded]` 7/1 (Windows real dump); `check_no_std_containers` 74; both validators PASS. Landed (a)–(k) in `crd::` containers. **(b) correlation wire format:** a sparse, appended CPROF `CorrelationRecord` side table (queue/device/clock-domain/uncertainty/pass/resource per sample), signalled by a flags bit — old readers ignore it, no `Sample` size bump. **(c) tracks:** per-(device,queue) tracks `"gpu d<dev> q<queue>"` (bounded `kMaxGpuTracks=16`, over-cap folds to an overflow track), `GpuSampleKind` Execution→Gpu / Transfer→Io / QueueWait→Wait. **(d) calibration + uncertainty:** `GpuClockCalibration` per device; a new leaf module `crd-perf-gpu-bridge` (`FrameGraphGpuBackend`) places passes that expose ticks and counts the rest unavailable — never fabricates a begin from `pass_gpu_ms` (a duration). **(i2):** both raster backends retain the raw tick pairs and override the getters (DX12 period 1e9/freq, valid_bits 64; Vulkan cached graphics-family `timestampValidBits`, actual on_async queue, async passes unavailable — their stamps are graphics-timeline). **(j) oracle:** placed total (span, gaps included) vs the graph's own `gpu_ms_total` — match/mismatch/incomparable, derived tolerance, duration-vs-duration in one domain, magnitude-only on mismatch. **(k) real end-to-end:** a real DX12/Vulkan frame graph through the real bridge places both passes with linked `pass_id` (**DX12 55/1 + Vulkan 53/1 on real hardware**); every acceptance clause has a written verdict (Session clause table) — Satisfied-real or Satisfied-crd-perf-with-named-follow-up (queue-wait / resource / device-loss *producers*, per-queue query pools for real async-queue placement, and a resolve-failure-vs-empty-frame signal are DIAG.7a-shaped provider work, not 6b gaps). | 079 | <a id="slice-diag.6c"></a>**DIAG.6c** | Needs CI | CPROF compatibility, Perfetto export and verified external sampling/unwind workflows; DG14. **(a) census landed** (2026-09-16): acceptance clauses + what-exists survey + sub-unit plan (b)–(i). Found: the CPROF v1 reader already rejects magic/version/size mismatch (tested `test_capture_roundtrip.cpp`); offline-symbolication identity exists (5d `symbol_index.hpp`); PDB in all shipping presets. Missing: Perfetto/chrome-trace export, an export CLI, a WPR/perf sampling runbook. This box HAS `wpr`/`xperf`/`wpaexporter`/`tracerpt` → the external-sampling + optimized-unwind proofs run **real** here (Linux `perf` stays documented). Open choice point: Perfetto wire format (JSON trace-events vs protobuf; JSON favored — offline, no service account, no dependency, matches the existing `to_json`). **(b) version discipline landed** (2026-09-16): the CPROF on-disk layout is now pinned field-by-field (`offsetof`+`sizeof` for every field of every on-disk struct in `test_diag_cprof_version.cpp`, teeth-verified via a compile-time `static_assert` failure), so a same-size reorder breaks the build before it changes bytes; reader rejects unknown version (v0/v2), byte-swapped magic and struct-size mismatch, and tolerates an unknown flag bit (additive forward-compat); procedure note at `kCprofVersion` records that 6b(b) consumed the header's last spare slot so the next optional section forces a v2 bump. Zero format bytes changed. win-debug `crd-perf-tests` 1256/196; `[cprof-version]` 22/4 (win-debug + win-asan); win-shipping compiles clean; my TU compiles clean under clang-cl-shipping (a pre-existing `-Winfinite-recursion` /WX defect in the DIAG.5d `crash_capture_specimen.cpp` dependency blocks that lane's full build — flagged separately, not 6c). **(c) Perfetto export landed** (2026-09-16): `export_perfetto_json` renders a `CaptureView` as offline Chrome/Perfetto trace-events JSON (scopes→X on pid 0 in µs; uncalibrated GPU→pid 1+device with RAW ticks, never rescaled; counters→typed C; frames on a reserved tid; M/i metadata + loss; `metadata` declares exporter/cprof_version/schema per ADR ID-1); JSON chosen over protobuf (offline, no dependency); all numbers integer-formatted (tr-TR locale-safe); `to_json` helpers hoisted to shared `json_writer.hpp` (zero behavior change). Observed: external `python json.load` gate → 10 events, ph={C,M,X}, pids=[0,8], numeric ts; teeth-verified (pid-domain break fails the test). win-debug `crd-perf-tests` 1280/197; `[perfetto]` 24/1 (win-debug + win-asan); win-shipping full link clean; clang-cl `crd-perf` (#else) clean. **(d) export/import acceptance landed** (2026-09-16): a test asserts native (`CaptureView`) == external (exported JSON) to the nanosecond — exact `"ts"/"dur"/"name"` fragments built from native `begin_ns`/`end_ns` via an independent µs-spec formatter, nesting (`outer.begin≤hot≤outer.end`, depth+1), counter `value:42` at the right frame, raw path exact; plus a container-free structural-JSON validator; teeth-verified (exporter ns-not-µs fails the equality). External trace_processor engine absent here → env-gated with the exact query command (Perfetto `slice.ts`==native `begin_ns` in ns). win-debug 1295/198; `[acceptance]` 16/1 (win-debug + win-asan); win-shipping clean. **(e) export CLI landed** (2026-09-16): `tools/cprof-export` → `cprof_export <in.cprof> <out.json>` wraps the same export path (links `crd-perf` only, not `crd-perf-ui`); new `load_capture_from_file` primitive; refuses with exit 3 when profiling is compiled out (never a zero-byte file — observed under clang-cl-shipping, which builds the CLI since a tools/ target has no crash-specimen dep); byte-identity oracle: `cprof_export` on the test's `.cprof` produced a json byte-IDENTICAL to the in-process export (`cmp` IDENTICAL, `json.load` OK). Registered (root `crd_module` + systems-map). win-debug + win-shipping + clang-cl-shipping build the CLI clean. **(f) external CPU-sampling runbook landed (artifacts + observed denial)** (2026-09-16): a scope-free hotspot specimen `crd-diag-cpu-hotspot-specimen` (`crd_diag_unscoped_hotspot_burn`, builds on win-debug + clang-cl-shipping), a verdict-machine runbook `scripts/sample-cpu-wpr.py` (wpr→xperf `-symbols -a profile -detail`→grep; try/finally `wpr -cancel`; local `_NT_SYMBOL_PATH` only), and `scripts/test-sample-cpu-wpr.py` 6/6. Observed: `wpr -start CPU` is DENIED here (`0xc5585011`, needs SeSystemProfilePrivilege) → runbook reports PERMISSION_DENIED (exit 3), no orphan session — clause 3 (hotspot visible via sampling) is **pending an elevated run the user performs**. **(g) optimized/fiber unwind qualification landed** (2026-09-16): the in-process oracle `tests/foundation/jobs/test_diag_unwind_qualification.cpp` (`[jobs][diag][unwind]`) records each function's `_ReturnAddress()` down a six-deep `noinline` chain (a `volatile` sink after every call defeats tail-call elision) and requires all appear in the `RtlCaptureStackBackTrace`/`backtrace` capture in **strictly increasing frame order** — a symbol-free, exact unwind oracle that reads the same `.pdata`/`.xdata` tables WPA/xperf do. Optimized native frames unwind correctly on win-debug + **win-shipping (`/O2`, the point — win-debug keeps frame pointers)** + **win-clang-cl-shipping** (41/41 each); the test lives in `crd-jobs-tests`, so flip-blocker 1's `crash_capture_specimen` defect does NOT gate the LTO lane here. crd-jobs fibers driven raw via the public `fiber_init_stack`/`fiber_switch` unwind their own frames AND **truncate at the hand-rolled switch boundary** (9 vs the thread's 24 frames, bounded — no runaway past the trampoline): the qualified limitation the acceptance demands, documented not silently dropped. Teeth (observed on `/O2`): force-inlining one frame collapses it → the strict-order `REQUIRE` fails (`out of order at index 4 (prev 4)`, exit 42) → restored + rebuilt → 41/41. External half: `scripts/sample-cpu-wpr.py --stacks` records the `Profile` stackwalk and processes `-a stack` (both verified against the installed `xperf -help`) with a new `MISSING_STACKS` verdict distinct from `HOTSPOT_NOT_VISIBLE`; `scripts/test-sample-cpu-wpr.py` **9/9**; observed PERMISSION_DENIED (`0xc5585011`) here, no orphan. **Clause 2 is SATISFIED in-process** on the shipping + clang-cl lanes; the external `-a stack` confirmation is elevation-gated and rides blocker (2). **(h) rich counters "where supported" landed** (2026-09-16): `scripts/counters-capability.py` is a capability/verdict machine recording each counter class (context_switch / page_fault / cache_branch_pmu / lock_contention / cpu_numa_affinity) as AVAILABLE / MULTIPLEXING_REQUIRED / PERMISSION_DENIED / UNAVAILABLE(reason) / TOOL_MISSING — **never a silent zero**, exactly the design clause ("record permissions, multiplexing and missing PMU events rather than treating unavailable counters as zero"). Unprivileged enumeration (`xperf -pmcsources` / `-providers KF`, syntax checked vs the installed help) runs here: all five classes AVAILABLE, the PMU is real (16 cache/branch sources, **max 8 concurrent** — one of the 9 profile slots is the timer, observed). Elevation-gated `--capture` observed on this box → PERMISSION_DENIED (NT Kernel Logger `0x5` / PMC-config), `--pmu-count 12` → MULTIPLEXING_REQUIRED, no orphaned session. Two defects caught by live observation (not recall): the N−1 timer-slot reservation, and a counter-count limit first mis-reported as PERMISSION_DENIED — now classified MULTIPLEXING_REQUIRED. `scripts/test-counters-capability.py` 11/11; clause SATISFIED as worded, the elevated capture riding blocker (2). **(i) frame pacing / input-to-present landed** (2026-09-16): `engine/foundation/perf/include/crd/perf/frame_pacing.hpp` — a pure, bounded `FramePacingAnalyzer` (fixed-bucket histograms, `crd::` only, integer ns) with THREE deliberately separate axes: pacing (frame-to-frame interval distribution — mean/p99/max/stutter-count), work (per-frame duration `end−begin`), and input-to-present latency (`present−input`). All computed from the **existing** `FrameRecord::frame_begin_ns`/`frame_end_ns`, so NO on-disk field is added and NO CPROF version bump is forced (the on-disk present/input timestamps are a named v2 follow-up, not smuggled into (i)); `feed_frames()` consumes a `CaptureView::frame_records()` span. `tests/foundation/perf/test_diag_frame_pacing.cpp` (`[perf][diag][pacing]`, 22 assertions / 4 cases) proves the SEPARATION the clause demands: equal throughput → different pacing tail (p99 ≥5 ms higher, stutter 50 vs 0); equal pacing → different work (4 vs 12 ms); input-to-present (48 ms, 3-frame pipeline) ≠ frame duration (16 ms). Teeth: making the histogram `bucket_of` ignore its value breaks the binning → cases 1 (pacing tail) & 3 (latency percentile) fail (exit 42), cases 2 & 4 pass — proving the histogram distinguishes the distributions; restored + rebuilt. Lanes: win-debug + win-asan + win-shipping (`/O2`) 22/22; the clang-cl TU compiles clean (`/W4 /WX -flto=thin`, object produced), the exe link riding blocker 1. Clause SATISFIED with no on-disk/version change. **(j) platform-debugger recipes landed** (2026-09-16): `scripts/debugger-recipes.py` carries recipes (in its header, script-is-runbook like (f)/(h)) mapping the clause's three targets to a debugger command AND an in-engine equivalent — uninstrumented work → WinDbg `!runaway` / lldb `break;run;bt` + the (f) WPR sampler; blocked time → `!analyze -v -hang` / `thread apply all bt` + the DIAG.4c hang watchdog/wait-graph, with the honest (g) cross-ref that a parked crd fiber sits on NO OS-thread stack (register-swap `fiber_switch`) so a debugger shows idle workers not the blocked fibers; memory pressure → `!address`/`!heap` / lldb `memory region` + the 3c DiagnosticAllocator + (h) `page_fault` — plus an availability verdict machine. Observed on this box (looked, not assumed): **lldb present but UNRUNNABLE** (`0xC0000135` STATUS_DLL_NOT_FOUND — `liblldb.dll` imports `python310.dll`, absent; box has Python 3.14), **cdb/windbg/gdb absent** → no runnable external interactive debugger, recorded first-class (never a faked session), cross-referenced to the engine's own crash/hang/allocator/counter diagnostics. `scripts/test-debugger-recipes.py` 6/6. Clause SATISFIED (recipes + in-engine equivalents; availability honest). **6c is now (a)–(j) COMPLETE — no agent-doable 6c work remains; it stays Open only on the two user-gated blockers below.** **Flip-blocking (BOTH must clear):** (1) the `crash_capture_specimen.cpp` clang-cl `-Winfinite-recursion` fix (`task_f28fe517`) so `crd-perf-tests` builds under clang-cl-shipping; (2) an elevated `sample-cpu-wpr.py` run confirming HOTSPOT_VISIBLE for the flat profile (clause 3) and `--stacks` call stacks (clause 2 external half). **User decision (2026-10-05):** the user performs one elevated `sample-cpu-wpr.py --stacks` run (blocker 2); blocker 1 was committed in 660a0857 and awaits CI confirmation; no hosted sampling lane. **Both blockers cleared (2026-10-07).** (1) The clang-cl `-Winfinite-recursion` fix is confirmed by the green complete-tier runs (nightly 37445373995, 37456628683, 37502078114): `win-clang-cl-shipping` builds `crd-perf-tests`. (2) The user's elevated WPR capture (process 26256, 18,194 samples) shows the unscoped hotspot through sampling: `HOTSPOT_VISIBLE` for the flat profile (clause 3) and for walked call stacks `__scrt_common_main → invoke_main → main → crd_diag_unscoped_hotspot_burn`, 4,093 inclusive and 2,635 exclusive samples (clause 2, external half). The capture also exposed a runbook defect, now fixed: `--stacks` exported with `xperf -a stack` and no activity, which xperf rejects (exit 5, empty output), so the run printed a failed export. The export now uses `-butterfly -process <specimen>`, the frame check accepts `module ! symbol`, and `--from-etl` re-analyses a recorded trace without elevation; both verdicts were reproduced from the user's trace that way. Six new verdict tests (15 in total) fail on the old script. **Hosted run must show** the tooling tests green; no hosted sampling lane (2026-10-05 decision). | [DIAG.6b](#slice-diag.6b) | [Detail](design/runtime-diagnostics.md#diag-6c); [ADR-0133](decisions/0133-runtime-diagnostics-and-instrumentation.md); [Session](sessions/2026-09-16-diag-6c-cprof-perfetto-external-sampling-census.md); [Nightly repairs](sessions/2026-10-04-nightly-ci-repairs.md); [Decisions 2026-10-05](sessions/2026-10-05-user-decisions.md); [User items 2026-10-07](sessions/2026-10-07-user-items-wpr-and-vulkan-loss.md); [Published CI](https://github.com/yatiyr/CRD/actions/runs/37445373995) |
| 080 | <a id="slice-diag.7a"></a>**DIAG.7a** | Partial | Unified provider validation lifecycle and stable object/pass/resource/program identities; DG11/DG12. **(a) census landed** (2026-09-16): acceptance clause verbatim + a two-backend what-exists survey + sub-unit plan (b)–(g). Found — validation capture ALREADY exists on BOTH backends (DX12 `ID3D12InfoQueue1`/`EnableDebugLayer`; Vulkan `crd::gpu::ValidationCapture` on a `VK_EXT_debug_utils` messenger), public headers already vendor-clean (`Dx12ValidationSeverity` / `ValidationSeverity`, crd:: containers — no `D3D12_*`/`Vk*`); DX12's clean-run predicate already treats **dropped messages as non-clean**; Vulkan images get SITE+SIZE debug-utils labels (8 call sites). Missing/parallel — the two backends' vocabularies are NOT unified (ADR's "one collector, no parallel" → unify into a common `crd::gpu` contract, which 7b/7c then extend); passes carry only STRING names (`add_pass(const char*)`), and there is NO stable Cerid resource/program/pass **ID + generation** attached to native object names/reports (only human site labels); no unified per-mode (core/sync/GPU-assisted) activation + unsupported-reason surface. Real GPU here → the acceptance (intentional lifetime/state/descriptor hazard → a **correlated** error on each claimed route; valid consumers stay clean; dropped ≠ clean) is a **device test observed on this box, both APIs**. Choice point recorded: identity "stable across" = reload/recreation (ID stable, generation bumps), mirroring the 3e handle/generation + 5d identity precedent — confirm vs DG12. **(b) common validation vocabulary landed** (2026-09-16): a backend-agnostic `crd::gpu` contract `engine/gpu/gpu-context/include/crd/gpu/validation.hpp` (`ValidationSeverity` + `ValidationReport{info,warning,error,dropped,truncated,instrumentation_failures; clean()}`) both captures project onto — `clean()` encodes the acceptance rule "dropped validation messages are not a clean run". Vulkan drops its local `ValidationSeverity` (now the common one — one definition, no ODR clash, every consumer keeps the `crd::gpu::ValidationSeverity` name) and **exposes the previously-unreachable `records_dropped`** via new `dropped_count()`/`report()` — closing a real gap: every Vulkan clean-run gate asserts only `error_count()==0 && warning_count()==0` (~40 sites, none bounds dropped/`messages().size()`), so a run that dropped messages would read as clean (structural — the gates cannot see drops; not a claim a hidden error was observed). DX12 keeps its rich native types (lifecycle 7b needs) + gains inline `to_common()` mappers (additive, no consumer breakage); the report mapper **folds instrument-failure lifecycle** — not-`Ready` / `device_creation_failures` / `execution_failures` → common `instrumentation_failures` — so a message-silent but never-started DX12 report maps to NON-clean (the registered-default-empty-reads-as-clean scar). Oracle `tests/gpu/gpu-context/test_diag_validation_vocab.cpp` (`[gpu][diag][validation][vocab]`, 10 assertions, device-free) + DX12 projection `[dx12][validation][vocab]` (13 assertions) in `test_dx12_validation.cpp`; teeth (win-debug): removing the `dropped==0` term fails the gpu-context dropped case (1/2) → 10/10; dropping the DX12 readiness/device/execution fold makes the not-`Ready` case read as clean → fails → restored + rebuilt → 13/13. Live per-backend dropped-injection on a real device is (g)'s acceptance specimen (`vkSubmitDebugUtilsMessageEXT` is in this box's SDK → feasible), not smuggled into (b). **(c) stable object identity value type landed** (2026-09-16): `engine/gpu/gpu-context/include/crd/gpu/object_identity.hpp` (header-only, no vendor types) defines `ObjectKind{Resource,Program,Pass}` + `struct ObjectIdentity{kind; u32 index; u32 generation; valid(); ==}` whose (index, generation) layout **mirrors `crd::containers::SlotMap::Handle` verbatim** (index 0xFFFFFFFF = null, generation 0 reserved, live generations start at 1 and bump on recreation — the alias-proof-on-reuse model the design demands: "IDs cannot silently alias when a GPU object is reused"). Name contract: `encode()` writes fixed-width ASCII lowercase hex `crd:<kind>:<8hex idx>:g<8hex gen>` (hand-formatted, locale-independent; REFUSES an invalid identity, returns 0), `parse()` scans for the token INSIDE a longer string (a debug-layer message quotes the name in prose) and accepts only a valid() token. Choice point resolved: the requirement rides the §7a clause + ADR schema clause 7 / record-schema (design L58-65, "IDs cannot silently alias … on reuse") = stable across reload/recreation, generation bumps (the DG tags live in the research doc; DG12 "GPU fault depth" is 7b/7c's, not a 7a identity driver — census corrects the (a) attribution). Routes: resource+program+PASS (vk) all proven. Remaining tail is 7b + PIX + the lifetime caveat, none a 7a correctness gap. (g-6, 2026-10-04) DX12 program route MEASURED: no Core error names a pipeline state (953 root-signature mismatch, 952, 933 carry no object text); the one naming record is warning 951 (Draw with a compute PSO), which carries the parsed Program id; [hazard] g-6 lands that warning-severity correlation with unnamed and valid legs. Error-severity program correlation on DX12 is a Core mechanism limit (7b GBV/DRED); whether warning severity satisfies the clause is part of the open user decision. **User decision (2026-10-05):** DIAG.7b(g) is the error-severity DX12 program proof. The lifetime class is built (retired-handle provenance with a use-after-destroy specimen on both APIs). The DX12 pass label uses the core BeginEvent/EndEvent marker, measured first and correlated through DRED contexts. No silent waivers. **Lifetime class proven** (2026-10-05) on both APIs: a destroyed or in-flight-released Cerid-named buffer yields an error correlated to its retired identity. On Vulkan it is the record-time error for an invalidated secondary command buffer (never submitted); on DX12 it is 921 OBJECT_DELETED_WHILE_STILL_IN_USE, with the held copy discarded by RemoveDevice. Controls are clean and the teeth hold. The premise that names are lost was measured false (VVL keeps them), so no handle table was built. **DX12 pass label emitted** (core BeginEvent/EndEvent carrying the pass identity, no PIX, wired around every frame-graph pass). The debug layer offers no local oracle (measured), so the pass-to-fault proof rides DRED contexts on the WARP real fault (7b h1). **DX12 pass route (2026-10-07):** the removal report now resolves the pass a stopped command list was inside from DRED's breadcrumb contexts (the innermost open marker carrying a Pass identity), proven on constructed lists with teeth. WARP produces no real fault (DIAG.7b(h1), measured), so the device proof moves to the user-run hardware fault DIAG.7b(h2), which asserts the faulting pass. **Remaining (user-only):** that (h2) run. All other routes are proven locally. **Hosted CI must show** the 7a cases green on the Windows lanes and on lavapipe. **DX12 pass-to-fault (2026-10-07):** the hardware proof is an explicitly unqualified route by user decision, because the user's GPU produced no real fault (DIAG.7b(h2), two runs). The pass resolver stays proven on constructed lists with teeth and on the WARP child. | 081 | <a id="slice-diag.7b"></a>**DIAG.7b** | Partial | DX12 GPU-based validation and DRED/device-removal evidence with capability/adapter qualification; DG12. **(a) census landed** (2026-10-04): debug layer, GBV enablement and removal observation exist; DRED setup/read, capability report, identity mapping, bundle and GBV qualification are missing; plan (b)-(h), simulated removal via RemoveDevice first, real device loss a separate hardware gate. **(b) landed** (2026-10-04): Dx12DeviceScope::request_dred sets breadcrumbs/page faults (+contexts) FORCED_ON/OFF before device creation, never inherited; DX12-local Dx12DredActivation reports NotRequested/Set/SettingsAbsent and the data interface; [dred] 22 assertions, [validation] 29 cases pass on hardware. **(c) landed** (2026-10-04): dx12_read_dred reads removal HRESULT, device state, breadcrumbs and page-fault allocations into a fixed report with per-query Ok/NotAvailable/Failed; simulated RemoveDevice: DRED on -> Ok, off -> NotAvailable, live -> NotAvailable. **(d) landed** (2026-10-04): stop_failed_device records every stopped device (Observed vs EngineForced) with its DRED report in a process-lifetime store; dx12_wait timeout test proves both origins. **(e) landed** (2026-10-04): breadcrumb list/queue and page-fault allocation names map to Cerid identities (retired kept); forced RemoveDevice measured to yield no breadcrumb nodes, so content needs (h); mapping proven on constructed DRED lists. **(f) landed** (2026-10-05): each recorded removal is also a DeviceRemoved live dump (gpu_*.dmp) with a versioned CRDR evidence bundle; dx12_read_removal_bundle reads it back or refuses NoStream/BadMagic/BadVersion/BadSize. **(g) landed** (2026-10-05): an uninitialized-descriptor compute dispatch on a Cerid-named pipeline reports DESCRIPTOR_UNINITIALIZED as an error naming its Program identity only under GBV (guarded, production submit/wait, device live); the core layer alone is silent; fixed a pre-existing GBV leak into no-request scopes (it faulted a later frame-graph test). Next (h) hardware-gated real fault. **User decision (2026-10-05):** (h) splits into (h1), a real page fault on WARP in a child process (labelled software evidence), and (h2), one opt-in hardware page-fault run started by the user. **(h1) measured (2026-10-07): WARP gives no real fault.** It discards stores to an unmapped GPU address (a released buffer, 1 GiB past a live one, a wild address) with the device live, and has no watchdog for a never-ending shader (55 s); the engine's forced removal then leaves DRED's breadcrumb list empty. The bounded child (`test_dx12_device_fault.cpp`) stays as the CI-capable WARP run of the whole path, labelled software evidence: it must end "completed" with no bundle, or "recorded" with the full bundle checked. The removal bundle is version 2: breadcrumb nodes keep their op history, context strings and the in-flight pass (teeth on the resolver and on the child). Lanes: win-debug (whole DX12 exe, 203 cases), win-shipping, win-clang-cl-shipping and win-asan. **Remaining (user-only): (h2)**, the real fault. On an idle machine, `set CRD_DX12_HARDWARE_FAULT_OPT_IN=1`, then `build\win-debug\tests\gpu-context-dx12\crd-gpu-context-dx12-tests.exe "[.dx12-hardware-device-fault]" -s`. It must show an observed removal with DRED state PageFault or Fault, the in-flight pass resolved to the faulting pass, and the released buffer among the recently freed allocations. **Hosted CI must show** `[dred]` green on the Windows lanes, with the (h1) WARP case passing. **User run and decision (2026-10-07): (h2) is an explicitly unqualified route.** On the user's RTX 4070 Ti SUPER the opt-in child completed both times (exit 30): first with the buffer released just before submission, then with the release flushed on the queue and a 500 ms pause. The device stayed live, and DRED recorded nothing. The driver does not turn a GPU use-after-free into a page fault, so a real DX12 fault bundle on hardware is listed as unproven, never claimed. The flush-and-pause hazard and the opt-in case stay. With (a)–(h1) done locally and not yet published, the row waits only for its first hosted run. | [DIAG.7a](#slice-diag.7a) | [Detail](design/runtime-diagnostics.md#diag-7b); [ADR-0133](decisions/0133-runtime-diagnostics-and-instrumentation.md); [Census](sessions/2026-10-04-diag-7b-census.md); [Decisions 2026-10-05](sessions/2026-10-05-user-decisions.md); [(h1) WARP measurement](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-7b-h1-warp-fault-and-pass-resolution.md); [User items 2026-10-07](sessions/2026-10-07-user-items-wpr-and-vulkan-loss.md) |
| 082 | <a id="slice-diag.7c"></a>**DIAG.7c** | Partial | Vulkan synchronization/GPU-assisted validation and supported device-fault evidence on Windows/Linux; DG12. **(a) census landed** (2026-10-05): DIAG.7a already qualifies mode activation, a core hazard, a sync missing-barrier WAW and a GPU-assisted out-of-bounds report after the fence. Still missing: the layer-absent path (it currently invalidates the context), layer/SDK version evidence, the instrumentation descriptor-slot fallback, bounded submit/wait with VK_ERROR_DEVICE_LOST retention, VK_EXT_device_fault collection (the workstation's RTX 4070 Ti SUPER advertises it), stale-resource cases and a loss bundle. Plan (b)-(h); a real loss stays a hardware gate, and hosted lanes have no Vulkan device. **(b) landed** (2026-10-05): the layer is enumerated before it is requested, and `validation_layer()` records presence and spec/implementation versions (VVL 1.4.341 here). With the layer disabled through the loader filter, a valid context reports LayerAbsent for every requested mode (previously the factory returned null). **(c) landed** (2026-10-05): bounded `vk_submit` and `vk_wait` classify each failure (Observed/TimedOut/Failed/Simulated) and keep the first one per device through later failures. A lost device is not called again, the last-known failure outlives the device, and a reused handle starts clean. The compute context latches invalid on failure. An injected loss and a contained timed-out wait are proven on hardware. The raster (present, upload batches, one-shots, frame graph), RT and DGC paths now submit and wait through the seam too, with `vk_queue_wait_idle`/`vk_device_wait_idle` for the unboundable idle waits; a fence or semaphore is waited on only when its submit succeeded, removing a latent infinite wait after a failed submit. **(d) landed** (2026-10-05): `VK_EXT_device_fault` is enabled when advertised (the RTX 4070 Ti SUPER; lavapipe lacks it), and the seam reads `vkGetDeviceFaultInfoEXT` once when a device is first recorded lost, into a fixed report (description, up to 8 address and 8 vendor infos, counts, the query result) kept as last-known state after the device is destroyed. On an injected loss the RTX driver answers `VK_ERROR_UNKNOWN` (the device is not really lost), which is recorded; a real loss stays the hardware gate. **User decision (2026-10-05):** a real loss follows 7b's (h1)/(h2) split; lavapipe on the hosted Linux lanes makes the Vulkan cases CI-run. **First lavapipe run** (2026-10-05) found four problems, all fixed and reproduced on the WSL reference host. (1) The 30 s bound abandoned in-flight work, so slow sorts segfaulted; `vk_wait_complete` now reports a slow completion and waits it out, proven with a timeline-semaphore gate. (2) The GLSL task shader did not pass a payload to a payload-declaring mesh (VUID-RuntimeSpirv-MeshEXT-10883). (3) The GPU-AV specimen executed its out-of-bounds store; GPU-AV now runs only in the layer's safe mode, and the specimen is memory-safe by construction. (4) The lifetime specimens executed against destroyed objects. B4-vis-2 was a real emitter defect: every compute dialect hoisted guarded buffer reads above their `if`, so empty pixels read far out of bounds; `KernelEmissionOrder` now keeps computed-address reads in order (five-dialect teeth). **(e) and (f) landed** (2026-10-07). (e): GPU-assisted instrumentation takes the device's last descriptor-set slot, and the layer does not lower the limit it reports. The context now reports the slot (`VulkanValidationLayer::instrumentation_set_slot`, `instruments(n)`), and the capture classifies the layer's GPU-AV setup warning as an instrumentation refusal, carried by the common `ValidationReport::instrumentation_failures`. On the RTX (limit 32), a 31-set layout runs the out-of-bounds specimen instrumented and correlated. A 32-set layout is refused at creation, and the same dispatch then reports nothing; shader objects behave the same. (f): a Cerid-named image cleared while still `UNDEFINED` is silent at record and yields an error correlated to its live identity by the fence wait, and the transitioned control is clean. The GPU-AV specimen asserts nothing before the wait. The destroyed-resource case is the DIAG.7a lifetime test. Teeth: classifier off, slot off by one, hazard leg transitioned. **(g) landed** (2026-10-07): the first time a device is recorded lost, after its fault report is read, the seam writes a pointer-free `VkLossRecord` (first failure, loss origin and operation, counts, adapter and driver, fault report) as a `DeviceRemoved` live dump with its own magic and version, once per device and never for a failure that is not a loss; `vk_read_loss_bundle` refuses foreign (DX12's included), other-version and other-size evidence. `lost()` now follows the first `VK_ERROR_DEVICE_LOST` even after an earlier timeout. On the RTX the injected loss round-trips (labelled Simulated, with the driver's fault-query result); on Linux the loss is kept in process and the write reports `Unsupported` (live dumps are Windows-only, DIAG.5a/5b). Teeth: write skipped, old `lost()` rule, reader version. **Remaining (user):** (h) per the 2026-10-05 split. Two loop steps on 2026-10-07 stopped before building the software leg, so (h) now waits for the user: decide whether and how the lavapipe child that measures a real `VK_ERROR_DEVICE_LOST` is built (h1), and run the opt-in hardware leg (h2) ([note](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-7c-h-handed-to-user.md)). **Hosted run must show** the (e), (f), (g) and DIAG.7a(g-3) cases passing on the lavapipe Linux lanes, with (g) reporting `Unsupported`; the hosted Windows lanes have no Vulkan device. **User decision (2026-10-07): (h) is an explicitly unqualified route.** No software (lavapipe) or hardware real-device-loss test is built. The loss path stands on the injected-loss, fault-report and loss-bundle proofs (c)–(g); a real `VK_ERROR_DEVICE_LOST` on a physical GPU remains unproven and is listed as such, never claimed. The DX12 counterpart, DIAG.7b(h2), is also an explicitly unqualified route: the user's two hardware runs completed without a fault. With (a)–(g) done locally and not yet published, the row waits only for its first hosted run (below). | [DIAG.7b](#slice-diag.7b) | [Detail](design/runtime-diagnostics.md#diag-7c); [ADR-0133](decisions/0133-runtime-diagnostics-and-instrumentation.md); [Census](sessions/2026-10-05-diag-7c-census.md); [Decisions 2026-10-05](sessions/2026-10-05-user-decisions.md) ; [(e)+(f)](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-7c-descriptor-limit-and-stale-layout.md); [(g)](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-7c-g-loss-bundle.md); [(h) to the user](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-7c-h-handed-to-user.md); [User items 2026-10-07](sessions/2026-10-07-user-items-wpr-and-vulkan-loss.md) |
| 083 | <a id="slice-diag.8a"></a>**DIAG.8a** | Partial | CHIR/CEIR/CKIR-compatible authored-source and lowering provenance through cook/optimization/reload; DG13. **CEIR core landed** (2026-10-07): measured first that the parser recorded no op location, `Operation::loc` is content (in `stable_hash`), passes dropped origins and plan errors were bare enums. Provenance is now a Context side table plus a skippable `ORIG` binary chunk, outside the content hash, CSE equality and the text (`crd/ceir/provenance.hpp`). `parse(ctx, text, file_id)` records each op's line:col and reports a parse error's line:col; CSE survivors and folds carry the union of the replaced ops' origins by stable id; the compiled plan owns per-instr sites, and `CompileResult::op`, `RunResult::fault` and the interpreter's `ExecResult::op` resolve to the authored line; unknown attribution is a typed gap. `test_provenance.cpp` (6 cases) runs text, CSE, serialize, fresh-Context load, compile and a BadForStep run to the authored `core.for` line, shows reformatting moves origins but no hash, and rejects a corrupt `ORIG`; teeth on CSE, `ORIG` and the fault latch. **Cook and reload landed** (2026-10-07): `cook_program_text(ctx, source, file, ...)` and `ReloadSet::add_source`/`reload_source(id, source, file)` cook under the named file, so the installed generation resolves runtime faults to `file:line:col`; a failed cook reports a POD `CookSite` (parse line:col, or the rejected op's authored origin, or a typed gap) copied out of the transient Context into `AddResult`/`ReloadResult`; the installed generation keeps its file through failed reloads, and a reformat-only `NoChange` reload moves its positions while keeping its handles. `test_reload_provenance.cpp` (3 cases) with teeth on file registration, the site fill, the copy-out and the refresh. **CHIR lowering landed** (2026-10-07): `lower_chir(m, ctx, file)` records a ChirNode origin (CHIR id plus line:col under the registered file, never the model's own file numbering) on every op it creates; the `core.state` cell names its StateDecl and the folded StateUpdate; graph-authored nodes keep their CHIR id with an explicit no-source-location gap, which `render_provenance` now lists. After CSE, serialization and a fresh-Context load, the committed program's outer-capture refusal (`CapturedValue`) and the interpreter's `UndefinedValue` name the authored `parallel_for` line; the self-contained variant compiles with every instr naming its node. `test_chir_provenance.cpp` (5 cases) with teeth on the fold, the file re-stamp and the render. **Host provider landed** (2026-10-07): measured first that the crd-jobs provider and the sequential reference blamed a parallel body, fold step or pooled launch body error on the owning `parallel_for`/`map_reduce`/`await` op while the plan named the inner instr. `Interpreter::failed_op()` now lets both blame the body op that raised the error (owner only when none), keeping first-in-index-order. `test_host_provenance.cpp` (4 cases) runs text, CSE, serialize and a fresh-Context load, then shows provider (job splits 1-8), reference and plan naming the same authored inner `core.for` line, index 0's loop B over program-first loop A, and the owner for its own fault; teeth on all four sites. **Entries, generations and CHIR reload landed** (2026-10-07): a lookup-failed `NoEntry` from the interpreter, the plan compiler and the crd-jobs provider carries an owned copy of the requested entry name (`entry`); `ReloadSet::locate` names the asset, the generation that ran a fault (matched by number: Current, Retiring or Gone), its content hash and the site rendered in that generation's own Context, so an old plan still running after a hot swap names generation 1 and its file while the new one names generation 2; a CHIR-lowered, cooked and installed program keeps its `ChirNode` origins (CHIR id, line:col, file) through a truncated blob, a contract change and an unparsable source, and a reformat-only CHIR reload moves them with the same ids. New cases in `test_provenance.cpp`, `test_host_provenance.cpp`, `test_reload_provenance.cpp` and `test_chir_reload_provenance.cpp` (2) with teeth on both entry copies, the install number, the generation match, the refresh gate and the refreshed space. **Intrinsic sites landed** (2026-10-07): `native_binding(ctx, op)` reads the ADR-0110 binding the registration already carries (`Intrinsic` with its declared provider, `NotIntrinsic`, or `Unregistered` when the Context cannot know, never reported as non-intrinsic), and `render_op_site` prints `<op> native <provider> at <file:line:col op#id>` for every reporter; the host scene resolver now blames every `UnresolvedSceneHandle` (unwired callback or 0 return) and `SceneChainMisuse` on the responsible resolve op (`SceneResolvedHandles::fault`). After text, CSE, serialize and a fresh-Context load, the reference `NoSemantics` and the plan's `UnsupportedOp` name `scene.resolve_material native host` at its authored line, and in a Context without the scene dialect the same op is `Unregistered`. New case in `test_provenance.cpp` and `tests/execution/ceir-gpu/test_scene_provenance.cpp` (2) with teeth on the intrinsic check, the refusal op and the misuse op. **GPU dispatch and CKIR record sites landed** (2026-10-07): with a `DispatchSites` table `execute_lowered` labels each recorded dispatch with a fresh Pass identity (`[<id>] compute.dispatch @<kernel>`, `ComputeRecorder::begin_label`/`end_label`, recorded on Vulkan) and the Vulkan capture keeps that label in `ValidationMessage::label` beside the object identity; a refused execute names the refused dispatch (`fault()`); a refused `.ckir` names its positional record (`CkirReadResult::pool`/`index`) and line:col. On the RTX 4070 Ti SUPER a Core `VUID-vkCmdDispatch-groupCountY-00387` raised by an authored over-limit grid (recorded, never submitted) maps through the label to the second `compute.dispatch` at its authored `file:line:col`, after text, CSE, serialize and load and again in a ReloadSet generation kept through a failed reload; measured that VVL 1.4.341 syncval reports no missing barrier between compute dispatches without `syncval_shader_accesses_heuristic`. `test_dispatch_provenance.cpp` (3), `test_dispatch_provenance_vulkan.cpp` (1) and a `test_ckir_asset.cpp` case, with teeth on the label, the capture's label field, the retire, the fault and the CKIR record. **CHIR graph-schema sites landed** (2026-10-07): `read_schema` returns `SchemaReadResult` (the `ChirParseResult` mold) naming the offending record's line and token column, the CHIR node it declares or names (with its stable id) and, for an orphaned layout row, the unresolved id; record fields must sit on the record's line, a second writer is blamed on the later edge, and whole-graph checks report in document order. On the committed `.chirgraph` with one authored mistake each (13 kinds), the named node's id leads to its line in the `.chir` text; teeth on the later-edge blame, the line-bound fields and the orphan line. **All local clauses are shown**; the row is not yet published, so it waits only for its first hosted run, which must show `crd-ceir-tests` (542 cases), `crd-ceir-cook-tests` (41), `crd-chir-tests` (29), `crd-ceir-host-tests` (29), `crd-ceir-gpu-tests` (137) and `crd-kir-tests` green on all six lanes with their `diag 8a` cases passing, and `crd-ceir-gpu-vulkan-tests`' `diag 8a` case passing on the lavapipe Linux lanes (it skips on the hosted Windows lanes, which have no Vulkan device). | [DIAG.7c](#slice-diag.7c) | [Detail](design/runtime-diagnostics.md#diag-8a); [ADR-0133](decisions/0133-runtime-diagnostics-and-instrumentation.md); [CEIR core](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-8a-ceir-provenance.md); [cook/reload](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-8a-cook-and-reload-provenance.md); [CHIR lowering](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-8a-chir-lowering-provenance.md); [host provider](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-8a-host-provider-provenance.md); [entries/generations/CHIR reload](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-8a-entries-generations-and-chir-reload.md); [intrinsic sites](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-8a-intrinsic-provider-sites.md); [GPU dispatch/CKIR sites](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-8a-gpu-dispatch-and-ckir-sites.md); [CHIR graph sites](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-8a-chir-graph-schema-sites.md) |
| 084 | <a id="slice-diag.8b"></a>**DIAG.8b** | Partial | Safe-point inspection/stepping and typed value snapshots through existing production execution; DG13. **Plan and interpreter safe points landed** (2026-10-07): measured first that the interpreter's §112 step hook had no user and let a held op run after a cancel, the compiled plan had no cancel and no slot types, stable ids are pre-order (they shift on insertion, so they cannot key a breakpoint) and no sandbox or headless consumer executes a plan or interpreter program. `plan::RunControl` (null-default, separate from the observation-only `RunHooks`) makes every instr a safe point with cancel checked before and after it (new `RunError::Cancelled`); `plan::read_value` reads a result only from the safe point's own frame (`Available`, `NotYetComputed`, `OutOfScope`, `OptimizedAway` naming the CSE survivor, `NoSuchValue`); the interpreter gains `call_depth()` and stops a held op on cancel. `crd/ceir/inspect.hpp`'s `Session` binds one generation, resolves authored `file:line` breakpoints through DIAG.8a provenance (`Bound`/`NoCodeAtLine`/`UnknownFile`), pauses the executing thread, answers typed snapshots (IR type, canonical text, quantity dimension, host redaction) from the paused thread, steps into/over/out, resumes and cancels, refuses stale-generation and unbound requests before any work, and types its scope (`Task`, `WholeHost` with host freeze/thaw, `NonPausable` counting hits) and the `SameThread` refusal for a safe point on the controller's own thread. `test_inspect.cpp` (6 cases) drives the plan and the interpreter from a second thread after text, CSE, serialize and a fresh-Context load; `test_reload_inspect.cpp` (1) rebinds a moved line across a ReloadSet hot swap and refuses the other generation; teeth on the breakpoint sites, the frame boundary, the generation check, cancel at a pause, the rebind, the call depth, redaction and the held instr. **Pending jobs landed** (2026-10-07): `HostProvider::execute(..., session)` gives the submitting interpreter the session's safe points through `inspect::HostLink`; the provider's cancel flag is the execution's only flag (`Session::cancel` raises it, so pooled bodies stop too, and a pause polls it, so `request_cancel` ends a paused run); each stop reports `pending_jobs` (pooled launches not yet joined); bodies the provider runs on its own sub-interpreters (pooled launch bodies, parallel ranges, fold steps) are detached: a breakpoint there is counted and refused `DetachedBody`, never paused. `test_host_inspect.cpp` (3 cases, `[ceir][host][diag]`; the test thread is the pool's thread 0 and executes, a second thread controls, a backstop cancels a run a broken path would hang): a stop with a pooled launch pending, resume joining it (pending 1 then 0, result 23), 13 detached hits with an in-frame launch pausing, and a session cancel and a provider cancel at the pause ending a pooled body that cannot finish; teeth on the link raise, the pause poll, the detached hook, the pending probe, the interpreter's flag and the session attach. **GPU seam landed** (2026-10-07): `execute_lowered(..., DispatchSites*, DeviceInspect*)` attaches its recording to a session (`Session::begin_device`, refused before any work: `NotBound`, `StaleGeneration`, `Busy`, returned as `ExecuteError::InspectRefused` with nothing recorded) and reports each dispatch as a device point that never waits whatever the session's scope: a breakpoint bound to the dispatch op is counted (`device_hits`) and refused `NonPausable`, a pause request during a recording is refused `NonPausable`, and a cancel stops the recording before the next dispatch (`ExecuteError::Cancelled`, blamed on that dispatch's authored line). Decided gaps: a recording is exclusive (a host op recording device work mid-execution is refused `Busy`), and `execute_rt_lowered`/`execute_work_lowered`/the render executor take no session. `test_dispatch_inspect.cpp` (3 cases, device-free; the recording runs on a second thread under a `Task` session with a backstop) and one real-device case in `test_dispatch_provenance_vulkan.cpp` (Task session, hit refused `NonPausable`, validation clean, outputs `a + 2b`); teeth on the hold path, the cancel, the generation check, the count, the pause refusal, the detach and the exclusivity. **Inspect host and headless consumer landed** (2026-10-07): crd-ceir-cook's `InspectHost` cooks an authored text into a ReloadSet generation, compiles one entry, binds the session and runs the plan on its own executing thread (the consumer's thread is the controller); every install and start rebinds (a replaced generation is refused `StaleGeneration`), a load or start during an execution is refused `Busy`, a rejected or failed reload keeps the last good generation, and the destructor cancels and joins a paused run; `inspect::op_at_line` names a value by its authored line. The committed authored program `assets/ceir/inspect_demo.ceir` (anti-drift against its builder) is run by the headless consumer `ceridc inspect` (CLI-only; the agent transports' authority is DIAG.8c), which reports every stop's authored line and depth, the watched lines' typed values (`available`, `not-yet-computed`, `out-of-scope`, with type text and unit) and the scripted steps, bounds its stops and waits, and validates the request before running. `test_inspect_host.cpp` (4 cases) and `test_ceridc_inspect.cpp` (3 cases, in process and through the real binary); teeth on the load rebind, the start rebind, the destructor cancel, the step actions and the stop bound. The `DeviceInspect::refusal` out field is now reset when no session refuses (tooth: the stale value survived). **Remaining (local):** the sandbox consumer: crd-sandbox runs an authored program through the same `InspectHost` with a non-blocking per-frame controller, loaded through the resolver's app-first program convention. | [DIAG.8a](#slice-diag.8a) | [Detail](design/runtime-diagnostics.md#diag-8b); [ADR-0133](decisions/0133-runtime-diagnostics-and-instrumentation.md); [plan/interpreter safe points](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-8b-safe-point-inspection.md); [pending jobs](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-8b-pending-jobs-and-detached-bodies.md); [GPU seam](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-8b-gpu-dispatch-non-pausable.md); [inspect host and headless consumer](sessions/2026-10-07-diag-8b-inspect-host-and-headless-consumer.md) |

## Verification and maintenance

Check this document with `python scripts/check-master-plan.py` from the repository root. It verifies the single
table, order, IDs, prerequisites, source routing and current documentation links; it does not test engine features.

Per slice: fulfill the linked contract, test the actual asset/runtime path, preserve last-good reload and record
quality/performance evidence. Local work uses changed modules plus blast radius, scoped CTest and incremental tidy;
whole-repository/configuration sweeps belong to CI. [BUILDING](BUILDING.md), [PRINCIPLES](PRINCIPLES.md) and
[SANITY](SANITY.md) remain binding. No performance claims without a saved full board in [bench](bench/README.md).

Update a row once with its evidence link; update a parent only when every child passes. Keep current focus as one
pointer in context. Sessions, ADRs, recipes, research and archives preserve knowledge; do not copy live Next lists
into them. A finding in the audit is assigned to a row here, not a new competing backlog. The user owns commits.
